### **Karmically Fruitful**
Tags: #karmically-fruitful #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kusala|Kusala]]** *(Skt: Kuśala)* — *Wholesome / Skillful / Healthy / Karmically Fruitful*
  * **Literal:** Skillful, intelligent, healthy, wholesome, pure
  * **Summary:** Thoughts, intentions, speech, and deeds that are rooted in non-greed (alobha), non-aversion (adosa), and non-delusion (amoha). They purify the mind, foster happiness, and support awakening.

### **Kilesa (Defilements**
Tags: #kilesa--defilements #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Defilements|Defilements]]** *(Skt: Kleśa)* — *Kilesa (Defilements / Mental Poisons)*
  * **Literal:** That which stains, torments, or soils
  * **Summary:** See Kilesa. Mental impurities that cloud the natural clarity of awareness and drive unwholesome karma.

### **Kilesas**
Tags: #kilesas #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kilesa|Kilesa]]** *(Skt: Kleśa)* — *Defilements / Mental Pollutants*
  * **Literal:** That which stains, soils, torments, or causes affliction
  * **Summary:** Mental states that obscure the innate clarity of consciousness and drive unwholesome actions. Rooted in Greed (lobha), Aversion (dosa), and Delusion (moha), expanding into secondary defilements like jealousy (issā), pride (māna), and deceit (māyā).

### **Kindness for suffering**
Tags: #kindness-for-suffering #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Karuṇā|Karuṇā]]** *(Skt: Karuṇā)* — *Compassion / Quivering of the Heart at Suffering*
  * **Literal:** That which quivers in response to suffering; active sympathy
  * **Summary:** The second of the Four Sublime Abodes (Brahmavihāras). The noble desire that suffering beings be freed from pain, accompanied by the courageous willingness to bear witness to suffering without aversion or despair.

### **Knowing heart**
Tags: #knowing-heart #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Buddho|Buddho]]** *(Skt: Buddha)* — *The One Who Knows / The Awake*
  * **Literal:** Awakened, enlightened; knowing
  * **Summary:** In the Thai Forest Tradition of Ajahn Mun and Ajahn Chah, 'Buddho' is the primary meditation word (parikamma) used to anchor the mind to the breath (Bud- on in-breath, -dho on out-breath). It also refers to the innate quality of pure, unconditioned knowing presence within the heart (citta).


## L

### **Last words of the Buddha**
Tags: #last-words-of-the-buddha #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Appamāda|Appamāda]]** *(Skt: Apramāda)* — *Heedfulness / Diligence / Vigilance*
  * **Literal:** Non-intoxication, non-negligence (a-pamāda)
  * **Summary:** Unflagging alertness, moral care, and earnest diligence in cultivating wholesome states and abandoning unwholesome ones. The Buddha declared appamāda to be the root of all wholesome qualities, and his dying words were: 'Vayadhammā saṅkhārā appamādena sampādetha' (All conditioned things are subject to decay; strive on with diligence).

### **Latent tendencies**
Tags: #latent-tendencies #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Anusaya|Anusaya]]** *(Skt: Anuśaya)* — *Latent Tendencies / Underlying Biases*
  * **Literal:** Lying dormant (anu-śī)
  * **Summary:** The seven deep-seated, dormant defilements that sleep in the subconscious mind until triggered by sensory contact: sensual passion (kāmarāga), aversion (paṭigha), views (diṭṭhi), doubt (vicikicchā), conceit (māna), desire for existence (bhavarāga), and ignorance (avijjā).

### **Latent Tendencies**

### **Liberated master**
Tags: #liberated-master #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Arahant|Arahant]]** *(Skt: Arhat)* — *Worthy One / Fully Enlightened Being*
  * **Literal:** One who is worthy (arahati) of homage, offerings, and reverence; one who has slain the defilements
  * **Summary:** A person who has eradicated all ten fetters (saṁyojana), completely destroyed all defilements (āsavas), attained full Nibbāna in this life, and ended the cycle of rebirth (saṁsāra).

### **Liberation**
Tags: #liberation #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nibbāna|Nibbāna]]** *(Skt: Nirvāṇa)* — *Unconditioned Peace / Extinguishment / Ultimate Liberation*
  * **Literal:** Extinguishment (of a fire due to lack of fuel), cooling down, unbinding (ni-vāna)
  * **Summary:** The ultimate goal of Buddhism. The complete extinguishing of the fires of greed (lobha), hatred (dosa), and delusion (moha). The unconditioned reality (asankhata-dhātu) free from birth, aging, death, and all forms of suffering.

### **Loving-kindness**
Tags: #loving-kindness #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Brahmavihāra|Brahmavihāra]]** *(Skt: Brahmavihāra)* — *Four Sublime Abodes / Immeasurables*
  * **Literal:** Divine / noble (brahma) abodes / dwellings (vihāra)
  * **Summary:** The four boundless virtues of the enlightened heart: (1) Mettā (Loving-kindness / Benevolence), (2) Karuṇā (Compassion for suffering), (3) Muditā (Sympathetic / Altruistic Joy), (4) Upekkhā (Equanimity / Impartiality). Radiated in all directions to all living beings without boundary.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Mettā|Mettā]]** *(Skt: Maitrī)* — *Loving-Kindness / Unconditional Goodwill*
  * **Literal:** Friendliness, benevolence, affectionate goodwill (from mitta, friend)
  * **Summary:** The first of the Four Sublime Abodes. The unconditional wish for the genuine welfare, peace, and happiness of all beings, without exception, preference, or expectation of return. Beautifully encapsulated in the Karaṇīya Mettā Sutta (Sn 1.8).

### **Loving-Kindness**
Tags: #loving-kindness #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Mettā|Mettā]]** *(Skt: Maitrī)* — *Loving-Kindness / Unconditional Goodwill*
  * **Literal:** Friendliness, benevolence, affectionate goodwill (from mitta, friend)
  * **Summary:** The first of the Four Sublime Abodes. The unconditional wish for the genuine welfare, peace, and happiness of all beings, without exception, preference, or expectation of return. Beautifully encapsulated in the Karaṇīya Mettā Sutta (Sn 1.8).

### **Luang Por**
Tags: #luang-por #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Luang Por|Luang Por]]** *(Skt: N/A (Thai))* — *Venerable Father / Respected Elder Monk*
  * **Literal:** Royal/Venerable (Luang) Father (Por)
  * **Summary:** In Thailand and the Western Thai Forest branch monasteries, an affectionate and deeply respectful title used for senior, beloved elder monks and lineage abbot figures.

### **Lucid Presence**
Tags: #lucid-presence #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sati|Sati]]** *(Skt: Smṛti)* — *Mindfulness / Lucid Presence / Recollection*
  * **Literal:** Memory, recollection, calling to mind, lucid awareness (smṛ)
  * **Summary:** The foundational mental faculty of non-judgmental, awake, present-moment awareness. The capacity to hold an object clearly in consciousness without distraction, reactivity, or forgetfulness. The primary engine of the Satipaṭṭhāna system.

### **Lust**
Tags: #lust #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kāmacchanda|Kāmacchanda]]** *(Skt: Kāmacchanda)* — *Sensual Desire / Craving for Pleasure*
  * **Literal:** Desire / yearning (chanda) for sensual pleasures (kāma)
  * **Summary:** The first of the Five Hindrances. The seductive mental pull toward pleasant sights, sounds, smells, tastes, touches, or romantic/sexual fantasies that scatters concentration and pulls awareness away from the present.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Rāga|Rāga]]** *(Skt: Rāga)* — *Passion / Lust / Attachment*
  * **Literal:** Coloring, dye, passion, intense desire (rañj)
  * **Summary:** Intense desire, lust, passion, and attachment that colors and stains consciousness, driving one to cling to sensory forms and conceptual identities.

### **Malevolence**
Tags: #malevolence #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vyāpāda|Vyāpāda]]** *(Skt: Vyāpāda)* — *Ill-Will / Malevolence / Aversion / Hostility*
  * **Literal:** Falling into ruin, injury, malice, malevolence
  * **Summary:** The second of the Five Hindrances. The aggressive, destructive desire to harm, hurt, punish, or reject another being or oneself. The inflamed manifestation of aversion (dosa).

### **Malice**
Tags: #malice #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vyāpāda|Vyāpāda]]** *(Skt: Vyāpāda)* — *Ill-Will / Malevolence / Aversion / Hostility*
  * **Literal:** Falling into ruin, injury, malice, malevolence
  * **Summary:** The second of the Five Hindrances. The aggressive, destructive desire to harm, hurt, punish, or reject another being or oneself. The inflamed manifestation of aversion (dosa).

### **Mantra**
Tags: #mantra #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Buddho|Buddho]]** *(Skt: Buddha)* — *The One Who Knows / The Awake*
  * **Literal:** Awakened, enlightened; knowing
  * **Summary:** In the Thai Forest Tradition of Ajahn Mun and Ajahn Chah, 'Buddho' is the primary meditation word (parikamma) used to anchor the mind to the breath (Bud- on in-breath, -dho on out-breath). It also refers to the innate quality of pure, unconditioned knowing presence within the heart (citta).

### **Master**
Tags: #master #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ajahn|Ajahn]]** *(Skt: Ācārya)* — *Teacher / Mentor (Thai / Pali)*
  * **Literal:** Master, spiritual guide, preceptor (Pali: Ācariya)
  * **Summary:** In the Thai Forest Tradition, a title of respect given to a senior monk who has spent at least ten rains retreats (vassa) in the robe and is qualified to guide students and train junior monastics.

### **Material phenomena**
Tags: #material-phenomena #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Rūpa|Rūpa]]** *(Skt: Rūpa)* — *Form / Matter / Physical Body*
  * **Literal:** Form, appearance, shape, that which changes and breaks down (ruppati)
  * **Summary:** The physical body and all material phenomena composed of the four great elements (mahābhūta): Earth (solid/cohesion), Water (fluid/cohesion), Fire (heat/temperature), and Air (movement/pressure). The first of the five aggregates.

### **Matter**
Tags: #matter #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Rūpa|Rūpa]]** *(Skt: Rūpa)* — *Form / Matter / Physical Body*
  * **Literal:** Form, appearance, shape, that which changes and breaks down (ruppati)
  * **Summary:** The physical body and all material phenomena composed of the four great elements (mahābhūta): Earth (solid/cohesion), Water (fluid/cohesion), Fire (heat/temperature), and Air (movement/pressure). The first of the five aggregates.

### **Meditation**
Tags: #meditation #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Bhavana|Bhavana]]** *(Skt: Bhāvanā)* — *Mental Cultivation / Meditation / Spiritual Development*
  * **Literal:** Causing to become, bringing into being, cultivation (bhāveti)
  * **Summary:** The systematic development of wholesome qualities of mind through meditation. Divided traditionally into Samatha-bhāvanā (cultivation of serenity and concentration) and Vipassanā-bhāvanā (cultivation of wisdom and insight).

### **Meditation anchor**
Tags: #meditation-anchor #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nāda|Nāda]]** *(Skt: Nāda)* — *The Sound of Silence / Inner Sound*
  * **Literal:** Sound, vibration, ringing sound, acoustic tone
  * **Summary:** A high-pitched, steady inner auditory vibration heard within awareness when the physical body and outer environment quiet down. Used in Buddhist and yogic meditation as an object of focus to cut through discursive mental chatter and enter spacious presence.

### **Meditation word**
Tags: #meditation-word #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Buddho|Buddho]]** *(Skt: Buddha)* — *The One Who Knows / The Awake*
  * **Literal:** Awakened, enlightened; knowing
  * **Summary:** In the Thai Forest Tradition of Ajahn Mun and Ajahn Chah, 'Buddho' is the primary meditation word (parikamma) used to anchor the mind to the breath (Bud- on in-breath, -dho on out-breath). It also refers to the innate quality of pure, unconditioned knowing presence within the heart (citta).

### **Meditative Absorption**
Tags: #meditative-absorption #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Jhāna|Jhāna]]** *(Skt: Dhyāna)* — *Meditative Absorption / Deep Stillness*
  * **Literal:** Burning up (defilements), contemplating, focused meditation
  * **Summary:** Deep, unified states of meditative absorption characterized by profound stillness, peace, and joy. The Four Form Rūpa-Jhānas progress through: (1) Initial application (vitakka), sustained application (vicāra), joy (pīti), bliss (sukha), and one-pointedness (ekaggatā); (2) Internal confidence, joy, bliss, one-pointedness; (3) Equanimous bliss; (4) Pure equanimity and purified mindfulness.

### **Meditative focus**
Tags: #meditative-focus #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Samādhi|Samādhi]]** *(Skt: Samādhi)* — *Concentration / Unification of Mind / Meditative Stillness*
  * **Literal:** Placing together, collecting, unifying (sam-ā-dhā)
  * **Summary:** The quality of mind that is collected, unified, steady, and undistracted. Cultivated through sustained mindfulness on a single meditation object (such as the breath, loving-kindness, or the 32 parts of the body). Culminates in the jhānas.

### **Meditative stillness**

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Samaṇa|Samaṇa]]** *(Skt: Śramaṇa)* — *Contemplative / Ascetic / Spiritual Seeker*
  * **Literal:** One who strives or toils spiritually (śram); one who has pacified the mind
  * **Summary:** A spiritual mendicant, ascetic, or contemplative who has left household life to pursue spiritual awakening through meditation, ethical purity, and simplicity.

### **Mental blindness**
Tags: #mental-blindness #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Moha|Moha]]** *(Skt: Moha)* — *Delusion / Confusion / Mental Blindness*
  * **Literal:** Delusion, bewilderment, infatuation, cloudiness
  * **Summary:** The third unwholesome root. The psychological state of cloudiness, confusion, and misapprehension that prevents one from seeing reality clearly, directly generating greed and hatred.

### **Mental Blindness**
Tags: #mental-blindness #english-to-pali

Tags: #mental-blocks #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Five Hindrances|Five Hindrances]]** *(Skt: Pañcanīvaraṇa)* — *Pañca Nīvaraṇā (Five Mental Obstacles)*
  * **Literal:** Five (pañca) coverings, obstacles, or hindrances (nīvaraṇa)
  * **Summary:** See Nīvaraṇa. (1) Kāmacchanda (Sensory Desire), (2) Vyāpāda (Ill-will/Aversion), (3) Thīna-middha (Sloth & Torpor), (4) Uddhacca-kukkucca (Restlessness & Worry), (5) Vicikicchā (Skeptical Doubt).

### **Mental concomitants**
Tags: #mental-concomitants #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Cetasika|Cetasika]]** *(Skt: Caitasika)* — *Mental Factors / Mental Concomitants*
  * **Literal:** Belonging to the mind (ceto / citta)
  * **Summary:** The 52 mental qualities that arise and pass away in conjunction with consciousness (citta). Classified into 7 universal, 6 occasional, 14 unwholesome (greed, anger, conceit, etc.), and 25 beautiful/wholesome factors (mindfulness, faith, non-greed, equanimity, wisdom).

### **Mental formations**
Tags: #mental-formations #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saṅkhāra|Saṅkhāra]]** *(Skt: Saṃskāra)* — *Mental Formations / Volitions / Conditioned Phenomena*
  * **Literal:** Formations, things put together, compounded realities (sam-kṛ)
  * **Summary:** A term with two major meanings: (1) The fourth aggregate (Saṅkhārakkhandha): volitional impulses, emotional formations, plans, and intentions that produce karma; (2) All compounded, conditioned things in the universe ('Sabbe saṅkhārā aniccā' - All conditioned things are impermanent).

### **Mental labels**

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saññā|Saññā]]** *(Skt: Saṃjñā)* — *Perception / Recognition / Mental Labels*

  * **Literal:** Coverings, obstacles, veils, barriers (ni-var)
  * **Summary:** The Five Hindrances (Pañca Nīvaraṇā) that blind mental vision, weaken wisdom, and obstruct concentration (samādhi): (1) Sensual Desire, (2) Ill-will, (3) Sloth and Torpor, (4) Restlessness and Worry, (5) Doubt.

### **Mental Poisons)**
Tags: #mental-poisons #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Defilements|Defilements]]** *(Skt: Kleśa)* — *Kilesa (Defilements / Mental Poisons)*
  * **Literal:** That which stains, torments, or soils
  * **Summary:** See Kilesa. Mental impurities that cloud the natural clarity of awareness and drive unwholesome karma.

### **Mental pollutants**
Tags: #mental-pollutants #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kilesa|Kilesa]]** *(Skt: Kleśa)* — *Defilements / Mental Pollutants*
  * **Literal:** That which stains, soils, torments, or causes affliction
  * **Summary:** Mental states that obscure the innate clarity of consciousness and drive unwholesome actions. Rooted in Greed (lobha), Aversion (dosa), and Delusion (moha), expanding into secondary defilements like jealousy (issā), pride (māna), and deceit (māyā).

  * **Literal:** Beautiful, noble, auspicious (kalyāṇa) friend (mitta)
  * **Summary:** A wise, compassionate spiritual guide, teacher, or companion on the path who inspires, mentors, and encourages wholesome living. When Ānanda remarked that spiritual friendship was half of the holy life, the Buddha corrected him: 'Spiritual friendship is the whole of the holy life.'

### **Merit**
Tags: #merit #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Puñña|Puñña]]** *(Skt: Puṇya)* — *Merit / Spiritual Goodness / Karmic Cleansing*
  * **Literal:** That which purifies and cleanses (pū)
  * **Summary:** The positive spiritual power and beneficial karmic energy generated through acts of generosity (dāna), ethical conduct (sīla), and mental cultivation/meditation (bhāvanā). Puñña purifies the mind, protects the practitioner, and creates favorable conditions for awakening.

### **Metta**

  * **Literal:** Friendliness, benevolence, affectionate goodwill (from mitta, friend)
  * **Summary:** The first of the Four Sublime Abodes. The unconditional wish for the genuine welfare, peace, and happiness of all beings, without exception, preference, or expectation of return. Beautifully encapsulated in the Karaṇīya Mettā Sutta (Sn 1.8).

### **Middle way**
Tags: #middle-way #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Aṭṭhaṅgika-magga|Aṭṭhaṅgika-magga]]** *(Skt: Aṣṭāṅgamārga)* — *Noble Eightfold Path*
  * **Literal:** Eight (aṭṭha) fold/limbed (aṅga) path (magga)
  * **Summary:** The Fourth Noble Truth. The middle way that avoids the extremes of sensual indulgence and self-mortification. Divided into three trainings: Wisdom (Right View, Right Intention), Ethics (Right Speech, Right Action, Right Livelihood), and Meditation (Right Effort, Right Mindfulness, Right Concentration).

### **Mind**
Tags: #mind #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Citta|Citta]]** *(Skt: Citta)* — *Mind / Heart / Consciousness / Core Awareness*
  * **Literal:** That which is conscious, that which thinks or knows; the heart-mind
  * **Summary:** The seat of awareness, emotion, and knowing in Buddhist psychology. In the Thai Forest Tradition, often translated as 'heart-mind'. In its conditioned state, it is soiled by visiting defilements (kilesas); in its radiant, purified state (pabhassara citta), it is liberated from all attachments.

### **Mindful Inquiry**
Tags: #mindful-inquiry #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Dhammavicaya|Dhammavicaya]]** *(Skt: Dharmapravicaya)* — *Investigation of Dhamma / Mindful Inquiry*
  * **Literal:** Investigation, discrimination, or deep scrutiny (vicaya) of phenomena/teachings (dhamma)
  * **Summary:** The second factor of enlightenment. The active, intelligent scrutiny of experience—investigating whether mental states are wholesome or unwholesome, examining how phenomena arise and cease, and discerning the three characteristics (anicca, dukkha, anattā).

### **Mindful walking**
Tags: #mindful-walking #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Cankama|Cankama]]** *(Skt: Caṅkramaṇa)* — *Walking Meditation*
  * **Literal:** Walking up and down a path, pacing
  * **Summary:** Formal walking meditation practiced along a straight path (usually 20 to 30 paces). Cultivates continuous mindfulness (sati) and clear comprehension (sampajañña) in the moving body.

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sati|Sati]]** *(Skt: Smṛti)* — *Mindfulness / Lucid Presence / Recollection*
  * **Literal:** Memory, recollection, calling to mind, lucid awareness (smṛ)
  * **Summary:** The foundational mental faculty of non-judgmental, awake, present-moment awareness. The capacity to hold an object clearly in consciousness without distraction, reactivity, or forgetfulness. The primary engine of the Satipaṭṭhāna system.

### **Mindfulness of body**
Tags: #mindfulness-of-body #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Satipaṭṭhāna|Satipaṭṭhāna]]** *(Skt: Smṛtyupasthāna)* — *Four Foundations of Mindfulness*
  * **Literal:** Establishing / stations (upaṭṭhāna) of mindfulness (sati)
  * **Summary:** The comprehensive meditation framework taught in the Satipaṭṭhāna Sutta (MN 10) and Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna Sutta (DN 22): (1) Kāyānupassanā: Mindfulness of the Body (breath, postures, 32 parts, elements, cemetery); (2) Vedanānupassanā: Mindfulness of Feelings (pleasant, unpleasant, neutral); (3) Cittānupassanā: Mindfulness of Mind (greed, hatred, delusion, expansive/contracted states); (4) Dhammānupassanā: Mindfulness of Phenomena (5 hindrances, 5 aggregates, 6 sense bases, 7 factors of awakening, 4 Noble Truths).

### **Mindfulness of breathing**
Tags: #mindfulness-of-breathing #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ānāpānasati|Ānāpānasati]]** *(Skt: Ānāpānasmṛti)* — *Mindfulness of Breathing*
  * **Literal:** Mindfulness (sati) of in-and-out breathing (āna-apāna)
  * **Summary:** The foundational meditation practice taught systematically by the Buddha in the Ānāpānasati Sutta (MN 118). It comprises sixteen steps divided across the Four Foundations of Mindfulness (Satipaṭṭhāna), cultivating both calm stability (samatha) and deep insight (vipassanā).

### **Mindfulness of Breathing**

  * **Literal:** Mindfulness (sati) of in-and-out breathing (āna-apāna)

### **Moha**
Tags: #moha #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Moha|Moha]]** *(Skt: Moha)* — *Delusion / Confusion / Mental Blindness*
  * **Literal:** Delusion, bewilderment, infatuation, cloudiness
  * **Summary:** The third unwholesome root. The psychological state of cloudiness, confusion, and misapprehension that prevents one from seeing reality clearly, directly generating greed and hatred.

### **Monastic**
Tags: #monastic #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Bhikkhu|Bhikkhu]]** *(Skt: Bhikṣu)* — *Monk / Fully Ordained Alms-gatherer*
  * **Literal:** One who shares or lives on alms (bhikkhati)
  * **Summary:** A male who has taken higher ordination (upasampadā) in the Theravada Buddhist monastic order, committing to live by the 227 rules of the Pāṭimokkha (Vinaya).
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Samaṇa|Samaṇa]]** *(Skt: Śramaṇa)* — *Contemplative / Ascetic / Spiritual Seeker*
  * **Literal:** One who strives or toils spiritually (śram); one who has pacified the mind
  * **Summary:** A spiritual mendicant, ascetic, or contemplative who has left household life to pursue spiritual awakening through meditation, ethical purity, and simplicity.

### **Monastic discipline**
Tags: #monastic-discipline #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vinaya|Vinaya]]** *(Skt: Vinaya)* — *Monastic Discipline / Code of Conduct*
  * **Literal:** Leading out, removing (defilements), training, discipline (vi-nī)
  * **Summary:** The first basket of the Tipiṭaka (Vinaya Piṭaka). The ethical discipline, procedural guidelines, and behavioral rules established by the Buddha for Buddhist monks and nuns to support harmony, simplicity, and liberation.

### **Monastic Discipline**
Tags: #monastic-discipline #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vinaya|Vinaya]]** *(Skt: Vinaya)* — *Monastic Discipline / Code of Conduct*
  * **Literal:** Leading out, removing (defilements), training, discipline (vi-nī)
  * **Summary:** The first basket of the Tipiṭaka (Vinaya Piṭaka). The ethical discipline, procedural guidelines, and behavioral rules established by the Buddha for Buddhist monks and nuns to support harmony, simplicity, and liberation.

### **Monastic elder**
Tags: #monastic-elder #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Thera|Thera]]** *(Skt: Sthavira)* — *Elder Monk*
  * **Literal:** Firm, steadfast, elder, senior
  * **Summary:** A fully ordained Buddhist monk who has completed at least ten rains retreats (vassa). A monk with twenty or more vassa is designated a Mahāthera (Great Elder).

### **Monastic order**
Tags: #monastic-order #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saṅgha|Saṅgha]]** *(Skt: Saṃgha)* — *Spiritual Community / The Monastic Order*
  * **Literal:** Community, assembly, flock, gathering together
  * **Summary:** The third of the Three Refuges. Operates on two levels: (1) Sammuti-saṅgha: The conventional ordained monastic community of monks (bhikkhus) and nuns (bhikkhunīs); (2) Ariya-saṅgha: The noble community of all beings (lay or monastic) who have attained at least the first stage of enlightenment (stream-entry).

### **Monastic rules**
Tags: #monastic-rules #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vinaya|Vinaya]]** *(Skt: Vinaya)* — *Monastic Discipline / Code of Conduct*
  * **Literal:** Leading out, removing (defilements), training, discipline (vi-nī)
  * **Summary:** The first basket of the Tipiṭaka (Vinaya Piṭaka). The ethical discipline, procedural guidelines, and behavioral rules established by the Buddha for Buddhist monks and nuns to support harmony, simplicity, and liberation.

### **Monk**
Tags: #monk #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Bhikkhu|Bhikkhu]]** *(Skt: Bhikṣu)* — *Monk / Fully Ordained Alms-gatherer*
  * **Literal:** One who shares or lives on alms (bhikkhati)
  * **Summary:** A male who has taken higher ordination (upasampadā) in the Theravada Buddhist monastic order, committing to live by the 227 rules of the Pāṭimokkha (Vinaya).

### **Monk title**
Tags: #monk-title #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ajahn|Ajahn]]** *(Skt: Ācārya)* — *Teacher / Mentor (Thai / Pali)*
  * **Literal:** Master, spiritual guide, preceptor (Pali: Ācariya)
  * **Summary:** In the Thai Forest Tradition, a title of respect given to a senior monk who has spent at least ten rains retreats (vassa) in the robe and is qualified to guide students and train junior monastics.

### **Moral causality**
Tags: #moral-causality #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kamma|Kamma]]** *(Skt: Karma)* — *Action / Volition / Intentional Deed*
  * **Literal:** Action, deed, work (kṛ)
  * **Summary:** The law of moral cause and effect. In Buddhism, kamma is explicitly defined by the Buddha as volition/intention (cetanā): 'Intention, O monks, is what I call kamma.' Wholesome actions yield pleasant, liberating fruits (vipāka); unwholesome actions yield suffering.

### **Moral Conduct**
Tags: #moral-conduct #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sīla|Sīla]]** *(Skt: Śīla)* — *Virtue / Moral Conduct / Ethical Discipline*
  * **Literal:** Habit, character, natural disposition, cooling moral integrity
  * **Summary:** The ethical foundation of all Buddhist practice. Encompasses harmlessness (ahiṁsā), purity of bodily action and speech. Expressed in the 5 Precepts (lay practitioners), 8 Precepts (upasampanna/retreatants), 10 Precepts (novices), and 227 Pāṭimokkha rules (monks).

### **Morality**
Tags: #morality #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sīla|Sīla]]** *(Skt: Śīla)* — *Virtue / Moral Conduct / Ethical Discipline*
  * **Literal:** Habit, character, natural disposition, cooling moral integrity
  * **Summary:** The ethical foundation of all Buddhist practice. Encompasses harmlessness (ahiṁsā), purity of bodily action and speech. Expressed in the 5 Precepts (lay practitioners), 8 Precepts (upasampanna/retreatants), 10 Precepts (novices), and 227 Pāṭimokkha rules (monks).

### **Mudita**
Tags: #mudita #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Muditā|Muditā]]** *(Skt: Muditā)* — *Sympathetic Joy / Altruistic Joy*
  * **Literal:** Joy in the happiness and success of others (from modati, to rejoice)
  * **Summary:** The third of the Four Sublime Abodes. The unselfish ability to genuinely rejoice in the good fortune, virtue, and success of other beings. The direct antidote to envy, jealousy (issā), and resentment.


## N

### **Nada**
Tags: #nada #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nāda|Nāda]]** *(Skt: Nāda)* — *The Sound of Silence / Inner Sound*
  * **Literal:** Sound, vibration, ringing sound, acoustic tone
  * **Summary:** A high-pitched, steady inner auditory vibration heard within awareness when the physical body and outer environment quiet down. Used in Buddhist and yogic meditation as an object of focus to cut through discursive mental chatter and enter spacious presence.

### **Nada sound**
Tags: #nada-sound #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nāda|Nāda]]** *(Skt: Nāda)* — *The Sound of Silence / Inner Sound*
  * **Literal:** Sound, vibration, ringing sound, acoustic tone
  * **Summary:** A high-pitched, steady inner auditory vibration heard within awareness when the physical body and outer environment quiet down. Used in Buddhist and yogic meditation as an object of focus to cut through discursive mental chatter and enter spacious presence.

### **Natural Law**
Tags: #natural-law #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Dhamma|Dhamma]]** *(Skt: Dharma)* — *Truth / Natural Law / Teaching of the Buddha / Phenomena*
  * **Literal:** That which upholds or sustains (dhar); truth; the way things are
  * **Summary:** A multi-layered term in Buddhism meaning: (1) The ultimate Truth or Law of Nature; (2) The teachings, discourses, and doctrine of the Buddha; (3) All conditioned and unconditioned phenomena/things (dhammā); (4) Moral duty and righteousness.

### **Negative states**
Tags: #negative-states #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Akusala|Akusala]]** *(Skt: Akuśala)* — *Unwholesome / Unskillful*
  * **Literal:** Not (a-) skillful, healthy, or intelligent (kusala)
  * **Summary:** Mental states, intentions, actions, and speech that are rooted in the three unwholesome roots: greed/attachment (lobha), aversion/hatred (dosa), and delusion/ignorance (moha). These states cause suffering and obstruct awakening.

### **Nibbana**
Tags: #nibbana #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nibbāna|Nibbāna]]** *(Skt: Nirvāṇa)* — *Unconditioned Peace / Extinguishment / Ultimate Liberation*
  * **Literal:** Extinguishment (of a fire due to lack of fuel), cooling down, unbinding (ni-vāna)
  * **Summary:** The ultimate goal of Buddhism. The complete extinguishing of the fires of greed (lobha), hatred (dosa), and delusion (moha). The unconditioned reality (asankhata-dhātu) free from birth, aging, death, and all forms of suffering.

### **Nirodha**
Tags: #nirodha #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nirodha|Nirodha]]** *(Skt: Nirodha)* — *Cessation / Quenching of Suffering*
  * **Literal:** Unbinding, cessation, confining, suppression (ni-rodha)
  * **Summary:** The Third Noble Truth: Dukkha-nirodha-ariyasacca. The complete cessation and ending of suffering through the relinquishment, letting go, and fading away of craving (taṇhā). Synonymous with Nibbāna.

### **Nirvana**
Tags: #nirvana #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nibbāna|Nibbāna]]** *(Skt: Nirvāṇa)* — *Unconditioned Peace / Extinguishment / Ultimate Liberation*
  * **Literal:** Extinguishment (of a fire due to lack of fuel), cooling down, unbinding (ni-vāna)
  * **Summary:** The ultimate goal of Buddhism. The complete extinguishing of the fires of greed (lobha), hatred (dosa), and delusion (moha). The unconditioned reality (asankhata-dhātu) free from birth, aging, death, and all forms of suffering.

### **No soul**
Tags: #no-soul #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Anattā|Anattā]]** *(Skt: Anātman)* — *Non-Self / Not-Self / Impersonality*
  * **Literal:** Not (an-) self / soul (attā)
  * **Summary:** The core Buddhist insight that all physical and mental phenomena (the five aggregates) are devoid of an enduring, autonomous, permanent ego, soul, or core entity. Things arise dependent on conditions, perform functions, and pass away without an owner.

### **Noble**
Tags: #noble #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ariya|Ariya]]** *(Skt: Ārya)* — *Noble / Spiritual Aristocrat*
  * **Literal:** Noble, exalted, honourable, pure
  * **Summary:** That which is spiritually noble, pure, and leading to liberation. Applied to the Four Noble Truths (Ariya-saccāni), the Noble Eightfold Path (Ariya-aṭṭhaṅgika-magga), and Noble Disciples (Ariya-sāvaka) who have entered the stream of awakening.

### **Noble Companion**
Tags: #noble-companion #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kalyāṇamitta|Kalyāṇamitta]]** *(Skt: Kalyāṇamitra)* — *Spiritual Friend / Noble Companion*
  * **Literal:** Beautiful, noble, auspicious (kalyāṇa) friend (mitta)
  * **Summary:** A wise, compassionate spiritual guide, teacher, or companion on the path who inspires, mentors, and encourages wholesome living. When Ānanda remarked that spiritual friendship was half of the holy life, the Buddha corrected him: 'Spiritual friendship is the whole of the holy life.'

### **Noble disciple**
Tags: #noble-disciple #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sotāpanna|Sotāpanna]]** *(Skt: Srotāpanna)* — *Stream-Enterer*
  * **Literal:** One who has entered (āpanna) the stream (sota) of the Dhamma
  * **Summary:** The first of the four stages of enlightenment (ariya-puggala). A stream-enterer has eradicated the first three fetters: (1) Sakkāyadiṭṭhi (Identity view), (2) Vicikicchā (Skeptical doubt in Triple Gem), (3) Sīlabbataparāmāsa (Clinging to rules/rituals as sufficient for liberation). Guaranteed to attain full Nibbāna in at most seven lifetimes, never falling into lower realms.

### **Noble Eightfold Path**
Tags: #noble-eightfold-path #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Aṭṭhaṅgika-magga|Aṭṭhaṅgika-magga]]** *(Skt: Aṣṭāṅgamārga)* — *Noble Eightfold Path*
  * **Literal:** Eight (aṭṭha) fold/limbed (aṅga) path (magga)
  * **Summary:** The Fourth Noble Truth. The middle way that avoids the extremes of sensual indulgence and self-mortification. Divided into three trainings: Wisdom (Right View, Right Intention), Ethics (Right Speech, Right Action, Right Livelihood), and Meditation (Right Effort, Right Mindfulness, Right Concentration).

### **Noble friend**
Tags: #noble-friend #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kalyāṇamitta|Kalyāṇamitta]]** *(Skt: Kalyāṇamitra)* — *Spiritual Friend / Noble Companion*
  * **Literal:** Beautiful, noble, auspicious (kalyāṇa) friend (mitta)
  * **Summary:** A wise, compassionate spiritual guide, teacher, or companion on the path who inspires, mentors, and encourages wholesome living. When Ānanda remarked that spiritual friendship was half of the holy life, the Buddha corrected him: 'Spiritual friendship is the whole of the holy life.'

### **Noble one**
Tags: #noble-one #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Anāgāmī|Anāgāmī]]** *(Skt: Anāgāmin)* — *Non-Returner*
  * **Literal:** One who does not return (an-āgāmin)
  * **Summary:** The third of the four stages of enlightenment (ariya-puggala). The non-returner has eradicated the first five lower fetters (saṁyojana), including sensual craving (kāmarāga) and ill-will (paṭigha). Upon physical death, an anāgāmī is reborn in the Pure Abodes (Suddhāvāsa) and attains Nibbāna there.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sakadāgāmī|Sakadāgāmī]]** *(Skt: Sakṛdāgāmin)* — *Once-Returner*
  * **Literal:** One who returns once (sakid-āgāmin)
  * **Summary:** The second stage of noble awakening (ariya-puggala). Having broken the first three fetters, the once-returner significantly attenuates sensual desire (kāmarāga) and ill-will (paṭigha). Will be reborn in the human realm at most one more time before attaining Nibbāna.

### **Noble path**
Tags: #noble-path #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ariya|Ariya]]** *(Skt: Ārya)* — *Noble / Spiritual Aristocrat*
  * **Literal:** Noble, exalted, honourable, pure
  * **Summary:** That which is spiritually noble, pure, and leading to liberation. Applied to the Four Noble Truths (Ariya-saccāni), the Noble Eightfold Path (Ariya-aṭṭhaṅgika-magga), and Noble Disciples (Ariya-sāvaka) who have entered the stream of awakening.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Magga|Magga]]** *(Skt: Mārga)* — *Path / Way*
  * **Literal:** Path, road, track, way leading to a goal
  * **Summary:** The spiritual path leading to liberation (Nibbāna), specifically the Noble Eightfold Path (Ariya-aṭṭhaṅgika-magga). In the Abhidhamma and stages of awakening, 'Magga' also refers to the four transcendent path-moments (Sotāpatti-magga, Sakadāgāmi-magga, Anāgāmi-magga, Arahatta-magga) that permanently cut fetters.

### **Noble truths**
Tags: #noble-truths #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ariya|Ariya]]** *(Skt: Ārya)* — *Noble / Spiritual Aristocrat*
  * **Literal:** Noble, exalted, honourable, pure
  * **Summary:** That which is spiritually noble, pure, and leading to liberation. Applied to the Four Noble Truths (Ariya-saccāni), the Noble Eightfold Path (Ariya-aṭṭhaṅgika-magga), and Noble Disciples (Ariya-sāvaka) who have entered the stream of awakening.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ariyasacca|Ariyasacca]]** *(Skt: Āryasatya)* — *The Four Noble Truths*
  * **Literal:** Noble (ariya) truths (sacca)
  * **Summary:** The fundamental framework of the Buddha's teaching: (1) Dukkha: The truth of suffering/unsatisfactoriness; (2) Samudaya: The origin of suffering in craving (taṇhā); (3) Nirodha: The cessation of suffering in Nibbāna; (4) Magga: The Noble Eightfold Path leading to the cessation of suffering.

### **Non-reactivity**
Tags: #non-reactivity #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Upekkhā|Upekkhā]]** *(Skt: Upekṣā)* — *Equanimity / Non-Reactivity / Balanced Seeing*
  * **Literal:** Looking over, observing impartially with balance (upa-īkṣ)
  * **Summary:** The fourth Sublime Abode, the seventh factor of enlightenment, and the tenth perfection. Serene, unshakeable mental balance that observes the arising and passing of all phenomena without attraction or aversion. Not cold indifference (its near enemy), but spacious, compassionate clarity.

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Anattā|Anattā]]** *(Skt: Anātman)* — *Non-Self / Not-Self / Impersonality*
  * **Literal:** Not (an-) self / soul (attā)
  * **Summary:** The core Buddhist insight that all physical and mental phenomena (the five aggregates) are devoid of an enduring, autonomous, permanent ego, soul, or core entity. Things arise dependent on conditions, perform functions, and pass away without an owner.

### **Novice ordination**
Tags: #novice-ordination #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Pabbajjā|Pabbajjā]]** *(Skt: Pravrajyā)* — *Going Forth / Novice Ordination*
  * **Literal:** Going forth from the household life to homelessness (pa-vraj)
  * **Summary:** The initial ordination ceremony in which a candidate renounces lay life, shaves their head, puts on monastic robes, and takes the Ten Precepts as a novice monk (sāmaṇera) or novice nun (sāmaṇerī).

### **Obstacles to meditation**
Tags: #obstacles-to-meditation #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Five Hindrances|Five Hindrances]]** *(Skt: Pañcanīvaraṇa)* — *Pañca Nīvaraṇā (Five Mental Obstacles)*
  * **Literal:** Five (pañca) coverings, obstacles, or hindrances (nīvaraṇa)
  * **Summary:** See Nīvaraṇa. (1) Kāmacchanda (Sensory Desire), (2) Vyāpāda (Ill-will/Aversion), (3) Thīna-middha (Sloth & Torpor), (4) Uddhacca-kukkucca (Restlessness & Worry), (5) Vicikicchā (Skeptical Doubt).

### **Obstructions to meditation**
Tags: #obstructions-to-meditation #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nīvaraṇa|Nīvaraṇa]]** *(Skt: Nīvaraṇa)* — *Hindrances / Mental Obstacles*
  * **Literal:** Coverings, obstacles, veils, barriers (ni-var)
  * **Summary:** The Five Hindrances (Pañca Nīvaraṇā) that blind mental vision, weaken wisdom, and obstruct concentration (samādhi): (1) Sensual Desire, (2) Ill-will, (3) Sloth and Torpor, (4) Restlessness and Worry, (5) Doubt.

### **Once-returner**
Tags: #once-returner #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sakadāgāmī|Sakadāgāmī]]** *(Skt: Sakṛdāgāmin)* — *Once-Returner*
  * **Literal:** One who returns once (sakid-āgāmin)
  * **Summary:** The second stage of noble awakening (ariya-puggala). Having broken the first three fetters, the once-returner significantly attenuates sensual desire (kāmarāga) and ill-will (paṭigha). Will be reborn in the human realm at most one more time before attaining Nibbāna.

### **Once-Returner**
Tags: #once-returner #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sakadāgāmī|Sakadāgāmī]]** *(Skt: Sakṛdāgāmin)* — *Once-Returner*
  * **Literal:** One who returns once (sakid-āgāmin)
  * **Summary:** The second stage of noble awakening (ariya-puggala). Having broken the first three fetters, the once-returner significantly attenuates sensual desire (kāmarāga) and ill-will (paṭigha). Will be reborn in the human realm at most one more time before attaining Nibbāna.

### **One-pointedness**
Tags: #one-pointedness #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Jhāna|Jhāna]]** *(Skt: Dhyāna)* — *Meditative Absorption / Deep Stillness*
  * **Literal:** Burning up (defilements), contemplating, focused meditation
  * **Summary:** Deep, unified states of meditative absorption characterized by profound stillness, peace, and joy. The Four Form Rūpa-Jhānas progress through: (1) Initial application (vitakka), sustained application (vicāra), joy (pīti), bliss (sukha), and one-pointedness (ekaggatā); (2) Internal confidence, joy, bliss, one-pointedness; (3) Equanimous bliss; (4) Pure equanimity and purified mindfulness.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Samādhi|Samādhi]]** *(Skt: Samādhi)* — *Concentration / Unification of Mind / Meditative Stillness*
  * **Literal:** Placing together, collecting, unifying (sam-ā-dhā)
  * **Summary:** The quality of mind that is collected, unified, steady, and undistracted. Cultivated through sustained mindfulness on a single meditation object (such as the breath, loving-kindness, or the 32 parts of the body). Culminates in the jhānas.

### **Open-handedness**
Tags: #open-handedness #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Dana|Dana]]** *(Skt: Dāna)* — *Generosity / Giving / Almsgiving*
  * **Literal:** Giving, gift, liberality
  * **Summary:** The practice of open-handed generosity without expecting a return. The first of the Ten Perfections (pāramī) and the initial step on the Buddhist spiritual path. Destroys stinginess (macchariya) and prepares the mind for concentration and wisdom.

### **Ordained woman**
Tags: #ordained-woman #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Bhikkhunī|Bhikkhunī]]** *(Skt: Bhikṣuṇī)* — *Nun / Fully Ordained Female Monastic*
  * **Literal:** Female alms-gatherer
  * **Summary:** A fully ordained female monastic in the Buddhist tradition, observing the rules of the Bhikkhunī Pāṭimokkha (311 rules in Theravada).


## P

### **Pain**
Tags: #pain #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Dukkha|Dukkha]]** *(Skt: Duḥkha)* — *Suffering / Unsatisfactoriness / Stress / Friction*
  * **Literal:** Difficult to bear (du-kham), poorly fitting axle-hole (friction, bumpiness)
  * **Summary:** The First Noble Truth. Encompasses all forms of physical pain, emotional grief, distress, dissatisfaction, and the existential friction of conditioned existence that fails to provide permanent security. Divided into: (1) Dukkha-dukkhatā (obvious physical/mental pain), (2) Vipariṇāma-dukkhatā (suffering due to change/impermanence), (3) Saṅkhāra-dukkhatā (pervasive unsatisfactoriness of conditioned formations).

### **Pali Canon**
Tags: #pali-canon #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Tipiṭaka|Tipiṭaka]]** *(Skt: Tripiṭaka)* — *The Pali Canon / Three Baskets*
  * **Literal:** Three (ti) baskets (piṭaka)
  * **Summary:** The complete collection of Buddhist scriptures preserved in the Pali language: (1) Vinaya Piṭaka: Monastic discipline and rules; (2) Sutta Piṭaka: Discourses of the Buddha and disciples (5 Nikāyas: Dīgha, Majjhima, Saṁyutta, Aṅguttara, Khuddaka); (3) Abhidhamma Piṭaka: Philosophical and psychological analysis.

### **Pali tradition**
Tags: #pali-tradition #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Theravāda|Theravāda]]** *(Skt: Sthaviravāda)* — *Way of the Elders / Ancient School*
  * **Literal:** Doctrine / discourse (vāda) of the Elders (thera)
  * **Summary:** The oldest surviving school of Buddhism, preserving the Pali Canon (Tipiṭaka). Predominant in Sri Lanka, Thailand, Myanmar, Cambodia, and Laos, and the source of the modern Thai Forest and Vipassanā movements worldwide.

### **Pali)**
Tags: #pali #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Ajahn|Ajahn]]** *(Skt: Ācārya)* — *Teacher / Mentor (Thai / Pali)*
  * **Literal:** Master, spiritual guide, preceptor (Pali: Ācariya)
  * **Summary:** In the Thai Forest Tradition, a title of respect given to a senior monk who has spent at least ten rains retreats (vassa) in the robe and is qualified to guide students and train junior monastics.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Tudong|Tudong]]** *(Skt: Dhūtāṅga (Pali))* — *Forest Wandering / Ascetic Pilgrimage (Thai / Pali)*
  * **Literal:** From Pali Dhutaṅga (means of shaking off defilements); wandering on foot
  * **Summary:** In the Thai Forest Tradition, the practice of wandering on foot through jungles, mountains, and villages with only bowl and umbrella-tent (glot), enduring wild beasts, weather, and hardships to deepen renunciation and meditation.

### **Paralyzing Indecision**
Tags: #paralyzing-indecision #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vicikicchā|Vicikicchā]]** *(Skt: Vicikitsā)* — *Skeptical Doubt / Paralyzing Indecision*
  * **Literal:** Doubt, uncertainty, wavering, perplexity
  * **Summary:** The fifth hindrance and second fetter. An agitated, wavering state of mental uncertainty regarding the path, the teachings, one's teacher, or one's own capacity to practice. Paralyzes spiritual progress by questioning endlessly without practicing.

  * **Literal:** Highest state, that which goes beyond (param)
  * **Summary:** The Ten Perfections cultivated across a spiritual lifetime: (1) Dāna (Generosity), (2) Sīla (Virtue/Morality), (3) Nekkhamma (Renunciation), (4) Paññā (Wisdom), (5) Viriya (Energy/Effort), (6) Khanti (Patience), (7) Sacca (Truthfulness), (8) Adhiṭṭhāna (Determination), (9) Mettā (Loving-kindness), (10) Upekkhā (Equanimity).

  * **Literal:** Allaying, calming down, quietude (pa-sambh)
  * **Summary:** The fifth factor of enlightenment. The deep physical and emotional relaxation and soothing that arises when the mind is freed from agitation and unwholesome states, preparing the way for one-pointed concentration (samādhi).

### **Path**
Tags: #path #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Magga|Magga]]** *(Skt: Mārga)* — *Path / Way*
  * **Literal:** Path, road, track, way leading to a goal
  * **Summary:** The spiritual path leading to liberation (Nibbāna), specifically the Noble Eightfold Path (Ariya-aṭṭhaṅgika-magga). In the Abhidhamma and stages of awakening, 'Magga' also refers to the four transcendent path-moments (Sotāpatti-magga, Sakadāgāmi-magga, Anāgāmi-magga, Arahatta-magga) that permanently cut fetters.

In the Abhidhamma and stages of awakening, 'Magga' also refers to the four transcendent path-moments (Sotāpatti-magga, Sakadāgāmi-magga, Anāgāmi-magga, Arahatta-magga) that permanently cut fetters.

### **Paticcasamuppada**
Tags: #paticcasamuppada #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Paṭiccasamuppāda|Paṭiccasamuppāda]]** *(Skt: Pratītyasamutpāda)* — *Dependent Origination / Interdependent Co-Arising*
  * **Literal:** Arising (samuppāda) dependent upon (paṭicca)
  * **Summary:** The fundamental Buddhist teaching explaining the causal mechanism of rebirth and suffering across 12 interdependent links: (1) Avijjā (Ignorance) ➔ (2) Saṅkhārā (Volitional Formations) ➔ (3) Viññāṇa (Consciousness) ➔ (4) Nāmarūpa (Mind & Form) ➔ (5) Saḷāyatana (Six Sense Bases) ➔ (6) Phassa (Contact) ➔ (7) Vedanā (Feeling) ➔ (8) Taṇhā (Craving) ➔ (9) Upādāna (Clinging) ➔ (10) Bhava (Becoming) ➔ (11) Jāti (Birth) ➔ (12) Jarāmaraṇa (Aging, Death, & Grief).

  * **Literal:** Patience, tolerance, enduring, bearing with
  * **Summary:** One of the Ten Perfections. The steadfast capacity to endure physical pain, heat and cold, harsh words, hunger, and difficult mental states without reacting with anger or giving up spiritual practice.

  * **Literal:** Five (pañca) bundles, heaps, or aggregates (khandha)
  * **Summary:** See Khandha. The five components that make up a human being: Form/Body (rūpa), Feeling (vedanā), Perception/Recognition (saññā), Mental Formations/Volition (saṅkhārā), and Consciousness (viññāṇa).

### **Paṭiccasamuppāda (Dependent Co-Arising)**

  * **Literal:** Arising (samuppāda) dependent upon (paṭicca)
  * **Summary:** See Paṭiccasamuppāda. The twelve-linked chain describing how ignorance (avijjā) gives rise to mental formations, consciousness, mind-and-matter, six senses, contact, feeling, craving, clinging, becoming, birth, and the entire mass of suffering.

### **Personality belief**

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sakkāyadiṭṭhi|Sakkāyadiṭṭhi]]** *(Skt: Satkāyadṛṣṭi)* — *Personality Belief / Identity View*
  * **Literal:** View (diṭṭhi) of an existing (sat) body/heap (kāya)
  * **Summary:** The erroneous core delusion that an enduring, independent 'self' or 'I' exists inside, owns, or controls the five aggregates. The first of the ten fetters, shattered completely upon entering the stream of awakening (Sotāpatti).

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Dhamma|Dhamma]]** *(Skt: Dharma)* — *Truth / Natural Law / Teaching of the Buddha / Phenomena*
  * **Literal:** That which upholds or sustains (dhar); truth; the way things are
  * **Summary:** A multi-layered term in Buddhism meaning: (1) The ultimate Truth or Law of Nature; (2) The teachings, discourses, and doctrine of the Buddha; (3) All conditioned and unconditioned phenomena/things (dhammā); (4) Moral duty and righteousness.

### **Philosophy**
Tags: #philosophy #english-to-pali

  * **Literal:** Higher or further (abhi) doctrine (dhamma)
  * **Summary:** The third basket (Piṭaka) of the Theravada Buddhist Canon, comprising a meticulous philosophical and psychological analysis of mind, consciousness (citta), mental factors (cetasika), matter (rūpa), and liberation (nibbāna).

### **Physical body**
Tags: #physical-body #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Rūpa|Rūpa]]** *(Skt: Rūpa)* — *Form / Matter / Physical Body*
  * **Literal:** Form, appearance, shape, that which changes and breaks down (ruppati)
  * **Summary:** The physical body and all material phenomena composed of the four great elements (mahābhūta): Earth (solid/cohesion), Water (fluid/cohesion), Fire (heat/temperature), and Air (movement/pressure). The first of the five aggregates.

### **Physical Body**

### **Piti**
Tags: #piti #english-to-pali

  * **Literal:** Joy, delight, refreshment (from prī, to please)
  * **Summary:** The fourth factor of enlightenment and the second factor of the First Jhāna. A buoyant, uplifting physical and energetic joy that arises as the mind lets go of sensual craving and enters sustained focus.

### **Pleasant unpleasant neutral**
Tags: #pleasant-unpleasant-neutral #english-to-pali

  * **Literal:** Feeling, sensing, experiencing (vid)

### **Pleasure**
Tags: #pleasure #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sukha|Sukha]]** *(Skt: Sukha)* — *Happiness / Bliss / Ease / Contentment*
  * **Literal:** Easy to bear (su-kham), well-fitting axle-hole (smooth ride)
  * **Summary:** Pleasant physical ease, mental happiness, deep emotional contentment, and blissful peace. The opposite of Dukkha. A crucial factor in the first three jhānas, fostering the stability needed for deep insight.

### **Poisons**

  * **Summary:** See Kilesa. Mental impurities that cloud the natural clarity of awareness and drive unwholesome karma.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kilesa|Kilesa]]** *(Skt: Kleśa)* — *Defilements / Mental Pollutants*
  * **Literal:** That which stains, soils, torments, or causes affliction
  * **Summary:** Mental states that obscure the innate clarity of consciousness and drive unwholesome actions. Rooted in Greed (lobha), Aversion (dosa), and Delusion (moha), expanding into secondary defilements like jealousy (issā), pride (māna), and deceit (māyā).

### **Pollutants of mind**
Tags: #pollutants-of-mind #english-to-pali

  * **Literal:** That which flows in or leaks out; intoxicating fermented brew
  * **Summary:** Deep, intoxicating pollutants of the mind that sustain the cycle of rebirth: (1) Kāmāsava (taint of sensuality), (2) Bhavāsava (taint of becoming/existence), (3) Diṭṭhāsava (taint of dogmatic views), (4) Avijjāsava (taint of ignorance). Their total destruction is the definition of Arahantship (āsavakkhaya).

### **Practice**
Tags: #practice #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Bhavana|Bhavana]]** *(Skt: Bhāvanā)* — *Mental Cultivation / Meditation / Spiritual Development*
  * **Literal:** Causing to become, bringing into being, cultivation (bhāveti)
  * **Summary:** The systematic development of wholesome qualities of mind through meditation. Divided traditionally into Samatha-bhāvanā (cultivation of serenity and concentration) and Vipassanā-bhāvanā (cultivation of wisdom and insight).

### **Prajna**
Tags: #prajna #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Paññā|Paññā]]** *(Skt: Prajñā)* — *Wisdom / Discernment / Direct Insight*
  * **Literal:** Knowing deeply, thoroughly, and comprehensively (pa-ñā)
  * **Summary:** The profound experiential realization of the true nature of reality—specifically understanding the Four Noble Truths, the Three Characteristics (anicca, dukkha, anattā), and Dependent Origination. The ultimate antidote to ignorance (avijjā).

### **Precepts**
Tags: #precepts #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sīla|Sīla]]** *(Skt: Śīla)* — *Virtue / Moral Conduct / Ethical Discipline*
  * **Literal:** Habit, character, natural disposition, cooling moral integrity
  * **Summary:** The ethical foundation of all Buddhist practice. Encompasses harmlessness (ahiṁsā), purity of bodily action and speech. Expressed in the 5 Precepts (lay practitioners), 8 Precepts (upasampanna/retreatants), 10 Precepts (novices), and 227 Pāṭimokkha rules (monks).

### **Presence**
Tags: #presence #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sati|Sati]]** *(Skt: Smṛti)* — *Mindfulness / Lucid Presence / Recollection*
  * **Literal:** Memory, recollection, calling to mind, lucid awareness (smṛ)
  * **Summary:** The foundational mental faculty of non-judgmental, awake, present-moment awareness. The capacity to hold an object clearly in consciousness without distraction, reactivity, or forgetfulness. The primary engine of the Satipaṭṭhāna system.

### **Present-moment awareness**
Tags: #present-moment-awareness #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sati|Sati]]** *(Skt: Smṛti)* — *Mindfulness / Lucid Presence / Recollection*
  * **Literal:** Memory, recollection, calling to mind, lucid awareness (smṛ)
  * **Summary:** The foundational mental faculty of non-judgmental, awake, present-moment awareness. The capacity to hold an object clearly in consciousness without distraction, reactivity, or forgetfulness. The primary engine of the Satipaṭṭhāna system.

### **Pride**
Tags: #pride #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Māna|Māna]]** *(Skt: Māna)* — *Conceit / Pride / Self-Comparison*
  * **Literal:** Measuring, comparing, pride, arrogance
  * **Summary:** The subtle ego-driven delusion of measuring oneself against others: 'I am better than', 'I am worse than' (inferiority conceit), or 'I am equal to'. It is one of the ten fetters, and is only completely eradicated at the stage of Arahantship.

### **Psychology**
Tags: #psychology #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Abhidhamma|Abhidhamma]]** *(Skt: Abhidharma)* — *Higher Teaching / Ultimate Doctrine*
  * **Literal:** Higher or further (abhi) doctrine (dhamma)
  * **Summary:** The third basket (Piṭaka) of the Theravada Buddhist Canon, comprising a meticulous philosophical and psychological analysis of mind, consciousness (citta), mental factors (cetasika), matter (rūpa), and liberation (nibbāna).

### **Punya**
Tags: #punya #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Puñña|Puñña]]** *(Skt: Puṇya)* — *Merit / Spiritual Goodness / Karmic Cleansing*
  * **Literal:** That which purifies and cleanses (pū)
  * **Summary:** The positive spiritual power and beneficial karmic energy generated through acts of generosity (dāna), ethical conduct (sīla), and mental cultivation/meditation (bhāvanā). Puñña purifies the mind, protects the practitioner, and creates favorable conditions for awakening.

### **Pure states**
Tags: #pure-states #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Kusala|Kusala]]** *(Skt: Kuśala)* — *Wholesome / Skillful / Healthy / Karmically Fruitful*
  * **Literal:** Skillful, intelligent, healthy, wholesome, pure
  * **Summary:** Thoughts, intentions, speech, and deeds that are rooted in non-greed (alobha), non-aversion (adosa), and non-delusion (amoha). They purify the mind, foster happiness, and support awakening.


## Q

### **Quenching**
Tags: #quenching #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nirodha|Nirodha]]** *(Skt: Nirodha)* — *Cessation / Quenching of Suffering*
  * **Literal:** Unbinding, cessation, confining, suppression (ni-rodha)
  * **Summary:** The Third Noble Truth: Dukkha-nirodha-ariyasacca. The complete cessation and ending of suffering through the relinquishment, letting go, and fading away of craving (taṇhā). Synonymous with Nibbāna.

### **Quenching of Suffering**
Tags: #quenching-of-suffering #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Nirodha|Nirodha]]** *(Skt: Nirodha)* — *Cessation / Quenching of Suffering*
  * **Literal:** Unbinding, cessation, confining, suppression (ni-rodha)
  * **Summary:** The Third Noble Truth: Dukkha-nirodha-ariyasacca. The complete cessation and ending of suffering through the relinquishment, letting go, and fading away of craving (taṇhā). Synonymous with Nibbāna.

### **Quivering of the Heart at Suffering**
Tags: #quivering-of-the-heart-at-suffering #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Karuṇā|Karuṇā]]** *(Skt: Karuṇā)* — *Compassion / Quivering of the Heart at Suffering*
  * **Literal:** That which quivers in response to suffering; active sympathy
  * **Summary:** The second of the Four Sublime Abodes (Brahmavihāras). The noble desire that suffering beings be freed from pain, accompanied by the courageous willingness to bear witness to suffering without aversion or despair.


## R

### **Radical inquiry**
Tags: #radical-inquiry #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Yoniso Manasikāra|Yoniso Manasikāra]]** *(Skt: Yoniśo Manaskāra)* — *Wise Attention / Appropriate Reflection / Radical Inquiry*
  * **Literal:** Attending (manasikāra) right from the origin/source (yoni)
  * **Summary:** The mental faculty of directing attention deeply to the root nature of experience—attending to impermanence rather than permanent stories, attending to not-self rather than ego dramas. The Buddha declared yoniso manasikāra to be the primary internal condition for the arising of Right View and Awakening.

### **Radical Inquiry**
Tags: #radical-inquiry #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Yoniso Manasikāra|Yoniso Manasikāra]]** *(Skt: Yoniśo Manaskāra)* — *Wise Attention / Appropriate Reflection / Radical Inquiry*

  * **Summary:** The mental faculty of directing attention deeply to the root nature of experience—attending to impermanence rather than permanent stories, attending to not-self rather than ego dramas. The Buddha declared yoniso manasikāra to be the primary internal condition for the arising of Right View and Awakening.

### **Raga**
Tags: #raga #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Rāga|Rāga]]** *(Skt: Rāga)* — *Passion / Lust / Attachment*
  * **Literal:** Coloring, dye, passion, intense desire (rañj)
  * **Summary:** Intense desire, lust, passion, and attachment that colors and stains consciousness, driving one to cling to sensory forms and conceptual identities.

### **Rapture**
Tags: #rapture #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Pīti|Pīti]]** *(Skt: Prīti)* — *Rapture / Joy / Spiritual Exhilaration*
  * **Literal:** Joy, delight, refreshment (from prī, to please)
  * **Summary:** The fourth factor of enlightenment and the second factor of the First Jhāna. A buoyant, uplifting physical and energetic joy that arises as the mind lets go of sensual craving and enters sustained focus.

### **Reality**
Tags: #reality #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sacca|Sacca]]** *(Skt: Satya)* — *Truth / Truthfulness / Reality*
  * **Literal:** That which is real, true, existent (sat)
  * **Summary:** Truthfulness, honesty, and integrity in speech and action; also the ultimate realities/truths of nature (e.g. Ariyasacca, the Four Noble Truths). One of the Ten Perfections.
* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Yathābhūta|Yathābhūta]]** *(Skt: Yathābhūta)* — *Seeing Things As They Truly Are*
  * **Literal:** According to (yathā) reality / what has become (bhūta)
  * **Summary:** Yathābhūtañāṇadassana: The direct, unclouded knowledge and vision of things exactly as they are in nature, free from the distortions of conceptual projection, wishful thinking, greed, or aversion.

### **Rebirth**
Tags: #rebirth #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saṁsāra|Saṁsāra]]** *(Skt: Saṃsāra)* — *Cycle of Rebirth / Wandering On*
  * **Literal:** Continuous wandering on, flowing together, wandering across realms (sam-sṛ)
  * **Summary:** The beginningless cycle of birth, death, and rebirth driven by karma, ignorance (avijjā), and craving (taṇhā). Characterized by inherent instability, suffering (dukkha), and lack of permanent refuge until Nibbāna is attained.

### **Recognition**
Tags: #recognition #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saññā|Saññā]]** *(Skt: Saṃjñā)* — *Perception / Recognition / Mental Labels*
  * **Literal:** Perceiving together, recognition, labeling (sam-ñā)
  * **Summary:** The third aggregate. The cognitive faculty that identifies, labels, categorizes, and remembers sensory data (e.g. recognizing 'blue', 'dog', 'danger', 'friend', 'pain').

### **Recollection**
Tags: #recollection #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sati|Sati]]** *(Skt: Smṛti)* — *Mindfulness / Lucid Presence / Recollection*
  * **Literal:** Memory, recollection, calling to mind, lucid awareness (smṛ)
  * **Summary:** The foundational mental faculty of non-judgmental, awake, present-moment awareness. The capacity to hold an object clearly in consciousness without distraction, reactivity, or forgetfulness. The primary engine of the Satipaṭṭhāna system.

### **Rejoicing in others**
Tags: #rejoicing-in-others #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Muditā|Muditā]]** *(Skt: Muditā)* — *Sympathetic Joy / Altruistic Joy*
  * **Literal:** Joy in the happiness and success of others (from modati, to rejoice)
  * **Summary:** The third of the Four Sublime Abodes. The unselfish ability to genuinely rejoice in the good fortune, virtue, and success of other beings. The direct antidote to envy, jealousy (issā), and resentment.

### **Relaxation**
Tags: #relaxation #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Passaddhi|Passaddhi]]** *(Skt: Praśrabdhi)* — *Tranquility / Serenity / Calming of Body and Mind*
  * **Literal:** Allaying, calming down, quietude (pa-sambh)
  * **Summary:** The fifth factor of enlightenment. The deep physical and emotional relaxation and soothing that arises when the mind is freed from agitation and unwholesome states, preparing the way for one-pointed concentration (samādhi).

### **Renunciation**
Tags: #renunciation #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Pabbajjā|Pabbajjā]]** *(Skt: Pravrajyā)* — *Going Forth / Novice Ordination*
  * **Literal:** Going forth from the household life to homelessness (pa-vraj)
  * **Summary:** The initial ordination ceremony in which a candidate renounces lay life, shaves their head, puts on monastic robes, and takes the Ten Precepts as a novice monk (sāmaṇera) or novice nun (sāmaṇerī).

### **Resentment**
Tags: #resentment #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Dosa|Dosa]]** *(Skt: Dveṣa)* — *Aversion / Hatred / Anger / Ill-Will*
  * **Literal:** Corrupting, burning, hurting, fault
  * **Summary:** One of the three primary poisons of the mind. Ranges from mild annoyance, irritation, impatience, and boredom to burning rage, hatred, resentment, and cruelty. It pushes away reality and desires destruction.

### **Resolute Resolve**
Tags: #resolute-resolve #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Adhiṭṭhāna|Adhiṭṭhāna]]** *(Skt: Adhiṣṭhāna)* — *Determination / Resolute Resolve*
  * **Literal:** Standing firm, establishing, foundation
  * **Summary:** One of the Ten Perfections (pāramī). An unshakeable, resolute decision and commitment to stay on the path of Dhamma, endure hardships, and fulfill spiritual goals regardless of obstacles.

### **Resolve**
Tags: #resolve #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Adhiṭṭhāna|Adhiṭṭhāna]]** *(Skt: Adhiṣṭhāna)* — *Determination / Resolute Resolve*
  * **Literal:** Standing firm, establishing, foundation
  * **Summary:** One of the Ten Perfections (pāramī). An unshakeable, resolute decision and commitment to stay on the path of Dhamma, endure hardships, and fulfill spiritual goals regardless of obstacles.

### **Respected Elder Monk**
Tags: #respected-elder-monk #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Luang Por|Luang Por]]** *(Skt: N/A (Thai))* — *Venerable Father / Respected Elder Monk*
  * **Literal:** Royal/Venerable (Luang) Father (Por)
  * **Summary:** In Thailand and the Western Thai Forest branch monasteries, an affectionate and deeply respectful title used for senior, beloved elder monks and lineage abbot figures.

### **Restlessness**
Tags: #restlessness #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Five Hindrances|Five Hindrances]]** *(Skt: Pañcanīvaraṇa)* — *Pañca Nīvaraṇā (Five Mental Obstacles)*
  * **Literal:** Five (pañca) coverings, obstacles, or hindrances (nīvaraṇa)
  * **Summary:** See Nīvaraṇa. (1) Kāmacchanda (Sensory Desire), (2) Vyāpāda (Ill-will/Aversion), (3) Thīna-middha (Sloth & Torpor), (4) Uddhacca-kukkucca (Restlessness & Worry), (5) Vicikicchā (Skeptical Doubt).

### **Right effort**
Tags: #right-effort #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Viriya|Viriya]]** *(Skt: Vīrya)* — *Energy / Effort / Spiritual Courage*
  * **Literal:** Vigor, heroic effort, manly energy (vīra)
  * **Summary:** The unflagging vigor, courage, and persistent application of energy in abandoning unwholesome states and cultivating wholesome states. Present in Right Effort (Sammā-vāyāma), the Seven Factors of Enlightenment, and the Ten Perfections.

### **Ripening**
Tags: #ripening #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vipāka|Vipāka]]** *(Skt: Vipāka)* — *Karmic Result / Fruit of Action*
  * **Literal:** Ripening, maturation, cooking (vi-pac)
  * **Summary:** The natural, inevitable fruition and psychological result of intentional actions (kamma). Wholesome intentions mature as peace and pleasant experience; unwholesome intentions mature as suffering.

### **Root of suffering**
Tags: #root-of-suffering #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Taṇhā|Taṇhā]]** *(Skt: Tṛṣṇā)* — *Craving / Thirst*
  * **Literal:** Thirst, feverish desire, craving (tṛṣ)
  * **Summary:** The Second Noble Truth: The root cause of suffering. The feverish thirst of the ego for experience: (1) Kāma-taṇhā: Craving for sensual pleasures; (2) Bhava-taṇhā: Craving for becoming, continued existence, and ego-expansion; (3) Vibhava-taṇhā: Craving for non-existence, annihilation, and pushing away painful reality.

### **Root reflection**
Tags: #root-reflection #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Yoniso Manasikāra|Yoniso Manasikāra]]** *(Skt: Yoniśo Manaskāra)* — *Wise Attention / Appropriate Reflection / Radical Inquiry*
  * **Literal:** Attending (manasikāra) right from the origin/source (yoni)
  * **Summary:** The mental faculty of directing attention deeply to the root nature of experience—attending to impermanence rather than permanent stories, attending to not-self rather than ego dramas. The Buddha declared yoniso manasikāra to be the primary internal condition for the arising of Right View and Awakening.


## S

### **Sakadagami**
Tags: #sakadagami #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sakadāgāmī|Sakadāgāmī]]** *(Skt: Sakṛdāgāmin)* — *Once-Returner*
  * **Literal:** One who returns once (sakid-āgāmin)
  * **Summary:** The second stage of noble awakening (ariya-puggala). Having broken the first three fetters, the once-returner significantly attenuates sensual desire (kāmarāga) and ill-will (paṭigha). Will be reborn in the human realm at most one more time before attaining Nibbāna.

### **Samadhi**
Tags: #samadhi #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Samādhi|Samādhi]]** *(Skt: Samādhi)* — *Concentration / Unification of Mind / Meditative Stillness*
  * **Literal:** Placing together, collecting, unifying (sam-ā-dhā)
  * **Summary:** The quality of mind that is collected, unified, steady, and undistracted. Cultivated through sustained mindfulness on a single meditation object (such as the breath, loving-kindness, or the 32 parts of the body). Culminates in the jhānas.

### **Samatha**
Tags: #samatha #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Samatha|Samatha]]** *(Skt: Śamatha)* — *Serenity / Calming / Tranquility Meditation*
  * **Literal:** Calmness, tranquility, stilling (śam)
  * **Summary:** The aspect of meditation practice focused on calming the mind, stilling discursive thought, pacifying the hindrances, and developing deep concentration (samādhi) and joy.

### **Samsara**
Tags: #samsara #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saṁsāra|Saṁsāra]]** *(Skt: Saṃsāra)* — *Cycle of Rebirth / Wandering On*
  * **Literal:** Continuous wandering on, flowing together, wandering across realms (sam-sṛ)
  * **Summary:** The beginningless cycle of birth, death, and rebirth driven by karma, ignorance (avijjā), and craving (taṇhā). Characterized by inherent instability, suffering (dukkha), and lack of permanent refuge until Nibbāna is attained.

### **Samyojana**
Tags: #samyojana #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saṁyojana|Saṁyojana]]** *(Skt: Saṃyojana)* — *Ten Fetters*
  * **Literal:** Bonds, shackles, yokes that bind one to saṁsāra (sam-yuj)
  * **Summary:** The ten shackles that bind consciousness to the wheel of rebirth: Lower Fetters: (1) Sakkāyadiṭṭhi (Identity view), (2) Vicikicchā (Doubt), (3) Sīlabbataparāmāsa (Attachment to rites/rituals), (4) Kāmarāga (Sensual craving), (5) Paṭigha (Ill-will); Higher Fetters: (6) Rūparāga (Craving for form realms), (7) Arūparāga (Craving for formless realms), (8) Māna (Conceit), (9) Uddhacca (Restlessness), (10) Avijjā (Ignorance).

### **Sangha**
Tags: #sangha #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saṅgha|Saṅgha]]** *(Skt: Saṃgha)* — *Spiritual Community / The Monastic Order*
  * **Literal:** Community, assembly, flock, gathering together
  * **Summary:** The third of the Three Refuges. Operates on two levels: (1) Sammuti-saṅgha: The conventional ordained monastic community of monks (bhikkhus) and nuns (bhikkhunīs); (2) Ariya-saṅgha: The noble community of all beings (lay or monastic) who have attained at least the first stage of enlightenment (stream-entry).

### **Sankhara**
Tags: #sankhara #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saṅkhāra|Saṅkhāra]]** *(Skt: Saṃskāra)* — *Mental Formations / Volitions / Conditioned Phenomena*
  * **Literal:** Formations, things put together, compounded realities (sam-kṛ)
  * **Summary:** A term with two major meanings: (1) The fourth aggregate (Saṅkhārakkhandha): volitional impulses, emotional formations, plans, and intentions that produce karma; (2) All compounded, conditioned things in the universe ('Sabbe saṅkhārā aniccā' - All conditioned things are impermanent).

### **Sanna**
Tags: #sanna #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Saññā|Saññā]]** *(Skt: Saṃjñā)* — *Perception / Recognition / Mental Labels*
  * **Literal:** Perceiving together, recognition, labeling (sam-ñā)
  * **Summary:** The third aggregate. The cognitive faculty that identifies, labels, categorizes, and remembers sensory data (e.g. recognizing 'blue', 'dog', 'danger', 'friend', 'pain').

### **Sati**
Tags: #sati #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sati|Sati]]** *(Skt: Smṛti)* — *Mindfulness / Lucid Presence / Recollection*
  * **Literal:** Memory, recollection, calling to mind, lucid awareness (smṛ)
  * **Summary:** The foundational mental faculty of non-judgmental, awake, present-moment awareness. The capacity to hold an object clearly in consciousness without distraction, reactivity, or forgetfulness. The primary engine of the Satipaṭṭhāna system.

### **Satipatthana**
Tags: #satipatthana #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Satipaṭṭhāna|Satipaṭṭhāna]]** *(Skt: Smṛtyupasthāna)* — *Four Foundations of Mindfulness*
  * **Literal:** Establishing / stations (upaṭṭhāna) of mindfulness (sati)
  * **Summary:** The comprehensive meditation framework taught in the Satipaṭṭhāna Sutta (MN 10) and Mahāsatipaṭṭhāna Sutta (DN 22): (1) Kāyānupassanā: Mindfulness of the Body (breath, postures, 32 parts, elements, cemetery); (2) Vedanānupassanā: Mindfulness of Feelings (pleasant, unpleasant, neutral); (3) Cittānupassanā: Mindfulness of Mind (greed, hatred, delusion, expansive/contracted states); (4) Dhammānupassanā: Mindfulness of Phenomena (5 hindrances, 5 aggregates, 6 sense bases, 7 factors of awakening, 4 Noble Truths).

### **Satya**
Tags: #satya #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Sacca|Sacca]]** *(Skt: Satya)* — *Truth / Truthfulness / Reality*
  * **Literal:** That which is real, true, existent (sat)
  * **Summary:** Truthfulness, honesty, and integrity in speech and action; also the ultimate realities/truths of nature (e.g. Ariyasacca, the Four Noble Truths). One of the Ten Perfections.

### **Second aggregate**
Tags: #second-aggregate #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vedanā|Vedanā]]** *(Skt: Vedanā)* — *Feeling Tone / Affective Sensation*
  * **Literal:** Feeling, sensing, experiencing (vid)
  * **Summary:** The second aggregate. The immediate visceral feeling tone that arises with every sensory contact: (1) Sukha-vedanā (Pleasant), (2) Dukkha-vedanā (Unpleasant/Painful), (3) Adukkhamasukha-vedanā (Neutral/Neither-pleasant-nor-unpleasant).

### **Second Brahmavihara**
Tags: #second-brahmavihara #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Karuṇā|Karuṇā]]** *(Skt: Karuṇā)* — *Compassion / Quivering of the Heart at Suffering*
  * **Literal:** That which quivers in response to suffering; active sympathy
  * **Summary:** The second of the Four Sublime Abodes (Brahmavihāras). The noble desire that suffering beings be freed from pain, accompanied by the courageous willingness to bear witness to suffering without aversion or despair.

### **Second fetter**
Tags: #second-fetter #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vicikicchā|Vicikicchā]]** *(Skt: Vicikitsā)* — *Skeptical Doubt / Paralyzing Indecision*
  * **Literal:** Doubt, uncertainty, wavering, perplexity
  * **Summary:** The fifth hindrance and second fetter. An agitated, wavering state of mental uncertainty regarding the path, the teachings, one's teacher, or one's own capacity to practice. Paralyzes spiritual progress by questioning endlessly without practicing.

### **Second hindrance**
Tags: #second-hindrance #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Vyāpāda|Vyāpāda]]** *(Skt: Vyāpāda)* — *Ill-Will / Malevolence / Aversion / Hostility*
  * **Literal:** Falling into ruin, injury, malice, malevolence
  * **Summary:** The second of the Five Hindrances. The aggressive, destructive desire to harm, hurt, punish, or reject another being or oneself. The inflamed manifestation of aversion (dosa).

### **Second Noble Truth**
Tags: #second-noble-truth #english-to-pali

* 👉 **[[Pali to English Dictionary#Taṇhā|Taṇhā]]** *(Skt: Tṛṣṇā)* — *Craving / Thirst*

  * **Summary:** The Second Noble Truth: The root cause of suffering. The feverish thirst of the ego for experience: (1) Kāma-taṇhā: Craving for sensual pleasures; (2) Bhava-taṇhā: Craving for becoming, continued existence, and ego-expansion; (3) Vibhava-taṇhā: Craving for non-existence, annihilation, and pushing away painful reality.

### **Second stage of awakening**

  * **Literal:** One who returns once (sakid-āgāmin)
  * **Summary:** The second stage of noble awakening (ariya-puggala). Having broken the first three fetters, the once-returner significantly attenuates sensual desire (kāmarāga) and ill-will (paṭigha). Will be reborn in the human realm at most one more time before attaining Nibbāna.
